I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.

I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.

So, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.

Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.

Like if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.

It’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.

Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.

That’s the part that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped?\n\nPatient: Afterward I feel… embarrassed, mostly. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.

That’s the part that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped?\n\nPatient: Afterward I feel… embarrassed, mostly. Mostly I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself afterward.\n\nIt’s just frustrating because, like, with school stuff or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for, like, three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Mostly I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself afterward.\n\nIt’s just frustrating because, like, with school stuff or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for, like, three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve thought about exercising more sometimes, but not like in a planned “make up for it” way, I don’t think. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. Um… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. Like, I have definitely had the thought of, okay, tomorrow I should “be better” or eat less or something, but I don’t really have a whole system for it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I guess what made me come in is, like, when I start eating sometimes it’s like a switch flips. I’ll be telling myself, “Dennis, stop, you’re done,” but I just keep going anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can be totally normal earlier in the day, like doing class stuff or just messing around on my phone, and then once I start, it doesn’t feel like I’m really choosing anymore. That’s the part that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped?\n\nPatient: Afterward I feel… embarrassed, mostly. And kind of grossed out with myself, honestly. Like, I’ll just sit there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nIt bugs me because in other stuff, like school or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. Like I can focus on a project for hours and be really controlled about it, so then this makes me feel like… I don’t know, like I’m not actually in control the way I thought I was.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty is probably the word, or close to it. Mostly I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself afterward.\n\nIt’s just frustrating because, like, with school stuff or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for, like, three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve thought about exercising more sometimes, but not like in a planned “make up for it” way, I don’t think. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, like my grandma had this whole shelf of stuff in her bathroom when I was a kid, which I probably was not supposed to be looking at, but… yeah, I’ve never really thought of using anything like that after eating.\n\nDiuretics, I’m actually not totally sure what counts. Is that like water pills? I’ve heard people talk about them online, but I don’t have, like, a routine or anything with that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah. That’s actually the part that freaks me out the most, I think.

It’s like once I start eating that way, some switch flips, and even while I’m eating I’ll be thinking, Dennis, just stop already. Like, I’m not even enjoying it the whole time. But I keep going anyway.

And then my brain does this annoying thing where it’s like, okay, after this one thing I’ll stop, and then it’s another thing. Kind of like when I’m working on a project and I say “just one more tweak,” except with that I usually can actually stop eventually. With food, in those moments, it feels a lot harder to pull the brakes.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I guess what made me come in is, like, when I start eating sometimes it’s like a switch flips. I’ll be telling myself, “Dennis, stop, you’re done,” but I just keep going anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can be totally normal earlier in the day, like doing class stuff or just messing around on my phone, and then once I start, it doesn’t feel like I’m really choosing anymore. That’s the part that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped?\n\nPatient: Afterward I feel… embarrassed, mostly. And kind of grossed out with myself, honestly. Like, I’ll just sit there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nIt bugs me because in other stuff, like school or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. Like I can focus on a project for hours and be really controlled about it, so then this makes me feel like… I don’t know, like I’m not actually in control the way I thought I was.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty is probably the word, or close to it. Mostly I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself afterward.\n\nIt’s just frustrating because, like, with school stuff or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for, like, three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve thought about exercising more sometimes, but not like in a planned “make up for it” way, I don’t think. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, like my grandma had this whole shelf of stuff in her bathroom when I was a kid, which I probably was not supposed to be looking at, but… yeah, I’ve never really thought of using anything like that after eating.\n\nDiuretics, I’m actually not totally sure what counts. Is that like water pills? I’ve heard people talk about them online, but I don’t have, like, a routine or anything with that. So… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing, honestly. Like, what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch or something, and I just keep going.\n\nAnd the annoying part is, it’s not like I don’t notice. Part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already, like you’re not even hungry anymore. But then I’ll still be reaching for more. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every episode of eating like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.\n\nIt’s weird because in other parts of my life I’m pretty disciplined, like with school stuff and robotics, where if something’s not working you just troubleshoot it and fix the code or whatever. But with this, it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and that part really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening regularly over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. I mean, I’ve thought about exercising more sometimes, but not like in a planned “make up for it” way, I don’t think. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, like my grandma had this whole shelf of stuff in her bathroom when I was a kid, which I probably was not supposed to be looking at, but… yeah, I’ve never really thought of using anything like that after eating.\n\nDiuretics, I’m actually not totally sure what counts. Is that like water pills? I’ve heard people talk about them online, but I don’t have, like, a routine or anything with that. So… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing, honestly. Like, what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch or something, and I just keep going.\n\nAnd the annoying part is, it’s not like I don’t notice. Part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already, like you’re not even hungry anymore. But then I’ll still be reaching for more. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every episode of eating like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.\n\nIt’s weird because in other parts of my life I’m pretty disciplined, like with school stuff and robotics, where if something’s not working you just troubleshoot it and fix the code or whatever. But with this, it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and that part really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening regularly over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It kind of started early in the semester, or that’s when I really noticed it. The semester start was a mess because I was trying to get my schedule sorted and there was this whole thing with one of my classes moving rooms, which doesn’t matter, sorry—\n\nBut yeah, it’s been most weeks. Usually it’s like one night in a week, and sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or if there’s just a bunch of food sitting around the apartment. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to counteract the calories you consumed during these episodes by exercising or dieting the next day?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. Like, I have definitely had the thought of, okay, tomorrow I should “be better” or eat less or something, but I don’t really have a whole system for it.\n\nExercise-wise, not really. I mean, sometimes I walk around campus a lot anyway, but that’s just because parking is terrible and the buildings are all weirdly spread out. I’m not, like, going on some huge run after because of it.\n\nDieting the next day… maybe I sort of think about it, but I don’t know how often I actually do anything. It’s more like I feel bad and tell myself I should fix it, and then the day just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually the part that freaks me out the most, I think.\n\nIt’s like once I start eating that way, some switch flips, and even while I’m eating I’ll be thinking, Dennis, just stop already. Like, I’m not even enjoying it the whole time. But I keep going anyway.\n\nAnd then my brain does this annoying thing where it’s like, okay, after this one thing I’ll stop, and then it’s another thing. Kind of like when I’m working on a project and I say “just one more tweak,” except with that I usually can actually stop eventually. With food, in those moments, it feels a lot harder to pull the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, that’s kind of the whole thing that made me finally come in. Once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch and I just keep going, even while part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, stop already.\n\nAnd it’s weird because with other stuff I can usually talk myself down, like if I’m procrastinating or spending too long on a game or whatever, I can eventually be like, okay, enough. But with this, in the moment, it doesn’t feel like I’m the one deciding how much is enough.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I think it’s more like I can’t stop. I mean, I guess technically I’m still picking up the food, so it sounds like choices, but it doesn’t feel like normal choosing.

Like, what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch or something, and I just keep going.\n\nAnd the annoying part is, it’s not like I don’t notice. Part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already, like you’re not even hungry anymore. But then I’ll still be reaching for more. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every episode of eating like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.\n\nIt’s weird because in other parts of my life I’m pretty disciplined, like with school stuff and robotics, where if something’s not working you just troubleshoot it and fix the code or whatever. But with this, it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and that part really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening regularly over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It kind of started early in the semester, or that’s when I really noticed it. The semester start was a mess because I was trying to get my schedule sorted and there was this whole thing with one of my classes moving rooms, which doesn’t matter, sorry—\n\nBut yeah, it’s been most weeks. Usually it’s like one night in a week, and sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or if there’s just a bunch of food sitting around the apartment. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to counteract the calories you consumed during these episodes by exercising or dieting the next day?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. But I keep going anyway.\n\nAnd then my brain does this annoying thing where it’s like, okay, after this one thing I’ll stop, and then it’s another thing. With food, in those moments, it feels a lot harder to pull the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. But with this, in the moment, it doesn’t feel like I’m the one deciding how much is enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control how much you're eating during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop, or is it more that you're making choices to continue eating despite not wanting to?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like I can’t stop. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely faster. Like, not normal eating-fast, more like I’m barely even registering it.

A lot of times I’ll be standing at my desk, kind of between doing homework and loading up a game or something, and I’m just eating without really sitting down or tasting it. Then I look and it’s like… wait, that was full a few minutes ago. It’s honestly kind of embarrassing how quickly it’s just gone.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I guess what made me come in is, like, when I start eating sometimes it’s like a switch flips. I’ll be telling myself, “Dennis, stop, you’re done,” but I just keep going anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can be totally normal earlier in the day, like doing class stuff or just messing around on my phone, and then once I start, it doesn’t feel like I’m really choosing anymore. That’s the part that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped?\n\nPatient: Afterward I feel… embarrassed, mostly. And kind of grossed out with myself, honestly. Like, I’ll just sit there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nIt bugs me because in other stuff, like school or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. Like I can focus on a project for hours and be really controlled about it, so then this makes me feel like… I don’t know, like I’m not actually in control the way I thought I was.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty is probably the word, or close to it. Mostly I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself afterward.\n\nIt’s just frustrating because, like, with school stuff or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for, like, three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve thought about exercising more sometimes, but not like in a planned “make up for it” way, I don’t think. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, like my grandma had this whole shelf of stuff in her bathroom when I was a kid, which I probably was not supposed to be looking at, but… yeah, I’ve never really thought of using anything like that after eating.\n\nDiuretics, I’m actually not totally sure what counts. Is that like water pills? I’ve heard people talk about them online, but I don’t have, like, a routine or anything with that. So… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing, honestly. Like, what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch or something, and I just keep going.\n\nAnd the annoying part is, it’s not like I don’t notice. Part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already, like you’re not even hungry anymore. But then I’ll still be reaching for more. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every episode of eating like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.\n\nIt’s weird because in other parts of my life I’m pretty disciplined, like with school stuff and robotics, where if something’s not working you just troubleshoot it and fix the code or whatever. But with this, it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and that part really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening regularly over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It kind of started early in the semester, or that’s when I really noticed it. The semester start was a mess because I was trying to get my schedule sorted and there was this whole thing with one of my classes moving rooms, which doesn’t matter, sorry—\n\nBut yeah, it’s been most weeks. Usually it’s like one night in a week, and sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or if there’s just a bunch of food sitting around the apartment. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to counteract the calories you consumed during these episodes by exercising or dieting the next day?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. Like, I have definitely had the thought of, okay, tomorrow I should “be better” or eat less or something, but I don’t really have a whole system for it.\n\nExercise-wise, not really. I mean, sometimes I walk around campus a lot anyway, but that’s just because parking is terrible and the buildings are all weirdly spread out. I’m not, like, going on some huge run after because of it.\n\nDieting the next day… maybe I sort of think about it, but I don’t know how often I actually do anything. It’s more like I feel bad and tell myself I should fix it, and then the day just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually the part that freaks me out the most, I think.\n\nIt’s like once I start eating that way, some switch flips, and even while I’m eating I’ll be thinking, Dennis, just stop already. Like, I’m not even enjoying it the whole time. But I keep going anyway.\n\nAnd then my brain does this annoying thing where it’s like, okay, after this one thing I’ll stop, and then it’s another thing. Kind of like when I’m working on a project and I say “just one more tweak,” except with that I usually can actually stop eventually. With food, in those moments, it feels a lot harder to pull the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, that’s kind of the whole thing that made me finally come in. But with this, in the moment, it doesn’t feel like I’m the one deciding how much is enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control how much you're eating during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop, or is it more that you're making choices to continue eating despite not wanting to?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like I can’t stop. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I guess what made me come in is, like, when I start eating sometimes it’s like a switch flips. I’ll be telling myself, “Dennis, stop, you’re done,” but I just keep going anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can be totally normal earlier in the day, like doing class stuff or just messing around on my phone, and then once I start, it doesn’t feel like I’m really choosing anymore. That’s the part that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped?\n\nPatient: Afterward I feel… embarrassed, mostly. And kind of grossed out with myself, honestly. Like, I’ll just sit there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nIt bugs me because in other stuff, like school or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. Like I can focus on a project for hours and be really controlled about it, so then this makes me feel like… I don’t know, like I’m not actually in control the way I thought I was.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty is probably the word, or close to it. Mostly I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself afterward.\n\nIt’s just frustrating because, like, with school stuff or robotics, I’m usually pretty disciplined. I can stay focused and stick to what I’m doing, so when this happens it makes me feel like… how am I not able to control this one thing?\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s not every day or anything, but since early in the semester it’s been most weeks. Usually it’ll be one night, sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s just a bunch of food around the apartment.\n\nIt’s kind of annoying because the semester started out normal, like I was just trying to get my schedule figured out and all that, and then it became this pattern. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for, like, three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like exercising or vomiting?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve thought about exercising more sometimes, but not like in a planned “make up for it” way, I don’t think. And vomiting, no—like, I really hate throwing up, even when I’m sick.\n\nI guess I mostly just feel bad and then try to move on, though sometimes I get distracted thinking I should be more disciplined, like how I am with classes or when I’m working on robotics stuff. But yeah, I’m not sure I do anything specific to cancel it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since these eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I don’t really weigh myself that consistently, and the scale at my apartment is kind of… I don’t know if it’s even accurate because it’s this cheap one my roommate bought.\n\nI mean, my clothes don’t feel hugely different or anything. Maybe a little, but that could also just be laundry shrinking stuff—I have this hoodie that fits weird now, but it’s old and I wear it constantly. So… I haven’t noticed a big weight change, at least not one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict your food intake on days after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, sometimes the next morning I’m not that hungry, or I’ll tell myself I should eat “lighter,” but I don’t know if that counts as restricting.\n\nIt’s usually more like I get busy and forget breakfast, especially if I’m rushing to class. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, like my grandma had this whole shelf of stuff in her bathroom when I was a kid, which I probably was not supposed to be looking at, but… yeah, I’ve never really thought of using anything like that after eating.\n\nDiuretics, I’m actually not totally sure what counts. Is that like water pills? I’ve heard people talk about them online, but I don’t have, like, a routine or anything with that. So… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing, honestly. Like, what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch or something, and I just keep going.\n\nAnd the annoying part is, it’s not like I don’t notice. Part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already, like you’re not even hungry anymore. But then I’ll still be reaching for more. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every episode of eating like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.\n\nIt’s weird because in other parts of my life I’m pretty disciplined, like with school stuff and robotics, where if something’s not working you just troubleshoot it and fix the code or whatever. But with this, it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and that part really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening regularly over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It kind of started early in the semester, or that’s when I really noticed it. The semester start was a mess because I was trying to get my schedule sorted and there was this whole thing with one of my classes moving rooms, which doesn’t matter, sorry—\n\nBut yeah, it’s been most weeks. Usually it’s like one night in a week, and sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or if there’s just a bunch of food sitting around the apartment. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to counteract the calories you consumed during these episodes by exercising or dieting the next day?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. Like, I have definitely had the thought of, okay, tomorrow I should “be better” or eat less or something, but I don’t really have a whole system for it.\n\nExercise-wise, not really. I mean, sometimes I walk around campus a lot anyway, but that’s just because parking is terrible and the buildings are all weirdly spread out. I’m not, like, going on some huge run after because of it.\n\nDieting the next day… maybe I sort of think about it, but I don’t know how often I actually do anything. It’s more like I feel bad and tell myself I should fix it, and then the day just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually the part that freaks me out the most, I think.\n\nIt’s like once I start eating that way, some switch flips, and even while I’m eating I’ll be thinking, Dennis, just stop already. Like, I’m not even enjoying it the whole time. But I keep going anyway.\n\nAnd then my brain does this annoying thing where it’s like, okay, after this one thing I’ll stop, and then it’s another thing. Kind of like when I’m working on a project and I say “just one more tweak,” except with that I usually can actually stop eventually. With food, in those moments, it feels a lot harder to pull the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, that’s kind of the whole thing that made me finally come in. Once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch and I just keep going, even while part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, stop already.\n\nAnd it’s weird because with other stuff I can usually talk myself down, like if I’m procrastinating or spending too long on a game or whatever, I can eventually be like, okay, enough. But with this, in the moment, it doesn’t feel like I’m the one deciding how much is enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control how much you're eating during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop, or is it more that you're making choices to continue eating despite not wanting to?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like I can’t stop. I do that anyway sometimes, though. My roommate is one of those people who meal-preps everything in containers, which makes me feel like I should be more organized, but I’m definitely not.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern where I’m intentionally not eating after. It’s kind of hard for me to tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Like, not on purpose. Sometimes I skip breakfast, but I do that on normal days too if I’m running late or if I stayed up too late working on something.\n\nAfter one of those episodes, I might wake up feeling kind of gross or just not really interested in food, but I wouldn’t say I’m fasting exactly. I think of fasting as like… people doing it intentionally with apps or schedules or whatever. My roommate had this whole phase with an eating window, and he kept talking about black coffee, which sounded miserable.\n\nSo, I guess no, not really. Not in a planned way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or upset, or is it more about the food being available?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s kind of both? Like, it happens more on nights when I’ve had a project deadline or I’m stressed out from trying to finish something, and then if there’s food already there it’s way easier.\n\nLike if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll tell myself I’m just having a couple slices, and then somehow I’m in my room and I’ve eaten basically a whole small pizza, plus chips, and then cereal too. Which is… more than I meant to eat, and definitely more than my roommates would have for a normal dinner.\n\nIt’s weird because the food being around makes it happen, but it’s usually worse when I’m already stressed or wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is tied to how you eat or your weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I think about it that directly. Like, I definitely don’t feel great after I eat like that, but I don’t know if that means my whole self-worth is tied to it.\n\nWeight is weird because everyone at college talks about it in random ways, like going to the gym or gaining the “freshman fifteen” or whatever, and then you start comparing yourself without meaning to. My roommate has this scale in the bathroom, which I think is kind of unnecessary, but also I notice it every time I’m brushing my teeth.\n\nSo… maybe it affects how I feel about myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s the main thing. I haven’t really sorted that out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, like my grandma had this whole shelf of stuff in her bathroom when I was a kid, which I probably was not supposed to be looking at, but… yeah, I’ve never really thought of using anything like that after eating.\n\nDiuretics, I’m actually not totally sure what counts. Is that like water pills? I’ve heard people talk about them online, but I don’t have, like, a routine or anything with that. So… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing, honestly. Like, what really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch or something, and I just keep going.\n\nAnd the annoying part is, it’s not like I don’t notice. Part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already, like you’re not even hungry anymore. But then I’ll still be reaching for more. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after every episode of eating like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, maybe not “guilty” in the dramatic way every single time, but afterward I usually feel really embarrassed. Kind of grossed out with myself, honestly.\n\nIt’s weird because in other parts of my life I’m pretty disciplined, like with school stuff and robotics, where if something’s not working you just troubleshoot it and fix the code or whatever. But with this, it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and that part really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening regularly over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It kind of started early in the semester, or that’s when I really noticed it. The semester start was a mess because I was trying to get my schedule sorted and there was this whole thing with one of my classes moving rooms, which doesn’t matter, sorry—\n\nBut yeah, it’s been most weeks. Usually it’s like one night in a week, and sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or if there’s just a bunch of food sitting around the apartment. I’d say it’s been pretty steady for at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to counteract the calories you consumed during these episodes by exercising or dieting the next day?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. Like, I have definitely had the thought of, okay, tomorrow I should “be better” or eat less or something, but I don’t really have a whole system for it.\n\nExercise-wise, not really. I mean, sometimes I walk around campus a lot anyway, but that’s just because parking is terrible and the buildings are all weirdly spread out. I’m not, like, going on some huge run after because of it.\n\nDieting the next day… maybe I sort of think about it, but I don’t know how often I actually do anything. It’s more like I feel bad and tell myself I should fix it, and then the day just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually the part that freaks me out the most, I think.\n\nIt’s like once I start eating that way, some switch flips, and even while I’m eating I’ll be thinking, Dennis, just stop already. Like, I’m not even enjoying it the whole time. But I keep going anyway.\n\nAnd then my brain does this annoying thing where it’s like, okay, after this one thing I’ll stop, and then it’s another thing. Kind of like when I’m working on a project and I say “just one more tweak,” except with that I usually can actually stop eventually. With food, in those moments, it feels a lot harder to pull the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, that’s kind of the whole thing that made me finally come in. Once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch and I just keep going, even while part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, stop already.\n\nAnd it’s weird because with other stuff I can usually talk myself down, like if I’m procrastinating or spending too long on a game or whatever, I can eventually be like, okay, enough. But with this, in the moment, it doesn’t feel like I’m the one deciding how much is enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control how much you're eating during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop, or is it more that you're making choices to continue eating despite not wanting to?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like I can’t stop. I mean, I guess technically I’m still picking up the food, so it sounds like choices, but it doesn’t feel like normal choosing.\n\nWhat really made me come in is that once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some switch and I just keep going, even while part of me is thinking, Dennis, just stop already.\n\nIt’s kind of stupid, because with other things I can usually be like, okay, pause, think for a second. Like if I’m clicking through videos instead of doing homework, eventually I can shut the laptop. But with this, in the moment, it feels like that “stop” part of me is there, but it’s not in charge.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely faster. Like, not normal eating-fast, more like I’m barely even registering it.\n\nA lot of times I’ll be standing at my desk, kind of between doing homework and loading up a game or something, and I’m just eating without really sitting down or tasting it. Then I look and it’s like… wait, that was full a few minutes ago. It’s honestly kind of embarrassing how quickly it’s just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your eating during these episodes because you can't stop or because you're choosing to continue despite not wanting to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sorry, I know I kind of already said this, but it’s really more like I can’t stop. Like, I’m not sitting there thinking, “I want to keep doing this.” It’s more like once I start, some switch flips and I just keep going.\n\nAnd in my head I’ll literally be like, Dennis, stop already, this is enough. But my body’s still just… doing it. It doesn’t feel like a normal choice, if that makes sense."
  }
